# Sprint brief — Tickets MVP

## Goal

Implement the tickets feature in megaplan per the design in `docs/tickets.md`. Tickets are short, repo-scoped notes on issues/problems, captured by humans or by agents on a human's prompt, that get folded into epics and auto-addressed when the resolving epic completes.

**The design doc `docs/tickets.md` is the source of truth for the data model, CLI surface, file format, modes, codebase identity, and integration touchpoints. Read it first. This brief only adds sprint scoping — what's in, what's out, what success looks like.**

## In scope for this sprint

Everything required to make tickets usable end-to-end in both **local-only mode** and **cloud-configured mode**:

1. **Schema migration (cloud-only)**: add `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables; add `root_commit_sha` column to `codebases` with an index. Additive only — no changes to existing tables beyond the new column.
2. **`megaplan/store/db.py`**: CRUD helpers for the new tables, column allowlist entries, codebase resolution that prefers `root_commit_sha`.
3. **New module `megaplan/tickets/`**: the canonical local representation. Frontmatter read/write for `.megaplan/tickets/{ulid}-{slug}.md` files. Mode-aware dispatch (cloud-configured → file + DB; local-only → file only). Codebase identity resolution from cwd via `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`.
4. **CLI `megaplan ticket …`** with all verbs in `docs/tickets.md` § CLI: `new`, `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`, `unlink`, `addressed`, `dismiss`, `reopen`. `ticket new` prints only the ULID to stdout on success; `--json` on read commands. `source` auto-derived from `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env.
5. **Auto-address hook**: in the epic state-transition path, after an epic moves to `done`, call `megaplan.tickets.address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id)`. Implementation handles both modes per the design doc. Idempotent.
6. **Planner discovery integration**: when assembling context for new/refined epics, include open tickets for the current repo, ranked by `tag overlap with epic goal desc, created_at desc`. The planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`. Same data shape in both modes (DB query in cloud, file scan in local-only).
7. **Resident/agent prompt**: a short paragraph teaching the agent that `megaplan ticket new` exists and when to use it (out-of-scope observations during epic work, user-requested captures).
8. **Tests** covering both modes for: file ↔ DB round-trip, link/unlink, auto-address idempotence, codebase identity resolution (rename case, fresh-repo case), CLI happy paths.

## Out of scope (defer)

- **Offline queue / `.pending/` directory / `megaplan ticket sync`**. Write-through only for now; if the DB write fails in cloud mode, the CLI errors loudly and the user retries. The design doc describes the offline queue — explicitly **do not build it** this sprint. Build the minimal write-through path.
- Conflict detection on concurrent edits — last-write-wins is fine.
- Severity, assignment, comments, cross-repo tickets — explicitly excluded per the design doc.
- Anything described in the doc's **Open items** section.

## Success criteria

- `megaplan ticket new "title" -b "body"` works in a local-only repo and writes the `.md` file.
- The same command in a cloud-configured repo writes the `.md` file **and** inserts a `tickets` row, auto-registering the codebase if needed (with `root_commit_sha` populated).
- `megaplan ticket link <ticket> <epic> --resolves` updates both the file frontmatter and the DB join row.
- Marking an epic `done` flips the linked ticket's status to `addressed` and writes a `resolution_note` referencing the epic — in both modes.
- New epic creation surfaces open tickets for the repo in the planner's context, ranked.
- Tests pass for both modes.

## Notes for the planner

- Follow the **core-first principle**: `megaplan/tickets/` owns the capability; the DB layer is purely additive and only kicks in when a store is configured. Mode detection follows whatever mechanism megaplan core already uses (presence of a configured store) — do not invent a new one.
- The `tickets` concept is **distinct from `feedback`**. Do not reuse the `feedback` table; do not conflate their lifecycles.
- Vendor flags / FK conventions / migration patterns should match how existing tables are handled in `megaplan/store/db.py`. Read existing CRUD helpers before designing new ones.
- The auto-address hook integration point is the one place where you must touch existing epic state-transition code. Find the existing hook surface (where epic events are emitted on state change) and add the call there — don't invent a parallel transition path.

        Plan:
        ## Implementation Plan: Tickets MVP

## Overview
Add a repo-scoped tickets feature per `docs/tickets.md`. Build a new `megaplan/tickets/` module that owns the canonical local representation (`.megaplan/tickets/{ulid}-{slug}.md` with YAML frontmatter), wire mode-aware dispatch into the existing DB store, register a new `megaplan ticket …` CLI subcommand group, and inject one auto-address call into the existing epic state-transition path (`megaplan/store/db.py:960` and `megaplan/store/file.py:1054`). DB changes are additive: new `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables plus a `root_commit_sha` column on `codebases`. Local-only mode operates entirely on files; cloud-configured mode write-throughs to DB. **No offline `.pending/` queue this sprint** — DB write failure raises loudly.

Existing repo shape:
- Migrations live in `supabase/migrations/` (latest: `202605060001_resident_orchestration.sql`). New file should follow naming `2026MMDDHHMM_*.sql`.
- DB store: `megaplan/store/db.py` with column allowlists at line ~237; codebase CRUD already exists (`create_codebase`, `find_codebase`, `update_codebase`, lines 1779–1955).
- CLI: subparsers added in `megaplan/cli.py:1176+`.
- Epic completion sites: `megaplan/store/db.py:960` (DB) and `megaplan/store/file.py:1054` (file backend) — both gate on `changes.get("status") == "done"`. This is the auto-address hook surface.
- Planner context assembly: `megaplan/handlers/plan.py` (and supporting `megaplan/_core/`).

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit existing patterns (`megaplan/store/db.py`, `megaplan/store/file.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/plan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** the codebase CRUD block (`megaplan/store/db.py:1779-1955`) and column allowlists (`megaplan/store/db.py:237-238`) to mirror naming/return conventions for the new `tickets` / `ticket_epics` helpers.
2. **Read** the epic→done branches at `megaplan/store/db.py:960` and `megaplan/store/file.py:1054` — these are the two call sites for the auto-address hook (one per backend).
3. **Skim** `megaplan/cli.py` ~line 1256 (`epic` subparser) for the established subparser pattern; mirror it for `ticket`.
4. **Locate** where planner context for a new/refined epic is assembled (`megaplan/handlers/plan.py`) and identify the insertion point for "open tickets for repo".

### Step 2: Schema migration (`supabase/migrations/2026MMDDHHMM_tickets.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** a new migration file, additive only:
   - `ALTER TABLE codebases ADD COLUMN root_commit_sha text;` + `CREATE INDEX idx_codebases_root_commit_sha ON codebases(root_commit_sha);`
   - `CREATE TABLE tickets (id text PRIMARY KEY, codebase_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES codebases(id), title text NOT NULL, body text NOT NULL, tags text[] DEFAULT '{}', status text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'open' CHECK (status IN ('open','addressed','dismissed')), source text NOT NULL CHECK (source IN ('human','agent','post-mortem')), filed_by_actor_id text REFERENCES … , filed_in_turn_id text REFERENCES bot_turns(id), resolution_note text, created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), last_edited_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), addressed_at timestamptz, dismissed_at timestamptz);` plus an index on `(codebase_id, status)`.
   - `CREATE TABLE ticket_epics (ticket_id text REFERENCES tickets(id) ON DELETE CASCADE, epic_id text REFERENCES … , resolves_on_complete boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false, linked_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), linked_by_actor_id text, note text, PRIMARY KEY (ticket_id, epic_id));` plus an index on `epic_id`.
2. **Match** the FK style (uuid vs text, ON DELETE behavior, `now()` defaults) used by the latest existing migrations — verify by reading `202604300009_009_codebase_research.sql` or similar for actor / bot_turn FK column types.

### Step 3: Schema dataclasses (`megaplan/schemas/models.py` or `megaplan/schemas/sprint1.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `Ticket` and `TicketEpicLink` Pydantic/dataclass models matching the new tables; export from `megaplan/schemas/__init__.py`.
2. **Add** `root_commit_sha: str | None` to the existing `Codebase` model.

### Step 4: DB store CRUD (`megaplan/store/db.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** to the `_IDEMPOTENT_MUTATORS` set and module exports: `create_ticket`, `update_ticket`, `link_ticket_to_epic`, `unlink_ticket_from_epic`, `address_tickets_resolved_by_epic`.
2. **Extend** the column allowlists (`megaplan/store/db.py:237`): add entries for `tickets` and `ticket_epics`; add `root_commit_sha` to the `codebases` allowlist.
3. **Implement** CRUD methods modeled on the codebase block (`megaplan/store/db.py:1779-1955`):
   - `create_ticket(...)`, `load_ticket(id)`, `list_tickets(codebase_id=…, status=…, tag=…, epic_id=…)`, `update_ticket(id, **changes)`.
   - `link_ticket_to_epic(ticket_id, epic_id, resolves_on_complete, note=None)`, `unlink_ticket_from_epic(...)`, `list_ticket_epic_links(ticket_id=…|epic_id=…)`.
   - `address_tickets_resolved_by_epic(epic_id) -> list[str]`: single SQL update setting `status='addressed'`, `addressed_at=now()`, `resolution_note='Resolved by epic <epic_id> on <date>: <title>'` for tickets joined via `ticket_epics` with `resolves_on_complete=true` and current `status='open'`. Returns affected ticket IDs. Idempotent.
4. **Add** codebase resolution helper `resolve_codebase_by_root_sha(root_sha, owner, name, repo_url) -> codebase_id`: lookup by `root_commit_sha` first, fall back to `(owner,name)` and backfill `root_commit_sha`, else `create_codebase` with all four populated.

### Step 5: New module `megaplan/tickets/` (canonical local representation)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `megaplan/tickets/__init__.py` exporting the public API: `new`, `list_open`, `show`, `edit`, `link`, `unlink`, `mark_addressed`, `mark_dismissed`, `reopen`, `address_resolved_by_epic`.
2. **Create** `megaplan/tickets/identity.py`:
   - `repo_root_sha(cwd) -> str` via `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`; raises a clean error if there are no commits; if multiple roots, sorts and picks smallest.
   - `repo_owner_name(cwd) -> tuple[str|None, str]` from `git remote get-url origin` (best-effort; tolerate missing remote in local-only mode).
3. **Create** `megaplan/tickets/files.py`:
   - `tickets_dir(repo_root) -> Path` returning `<repo>/.megaplan/tickets/`.
   - `read_ticket_file(path) -> TicketDoc` and `write_ticket_file(path, doc)` using a YAML frontmatter parser (use stdlib + a minimal YAML — check if `pyyaml` is already a dep; otherwise use the project's existing frontmatter helper if any in `megaplan/_core/io.py`).
   - `slugify(title)` for filename stem; full path `{ulid}-{slug}.md`.
   - `iter_ticket_files(repo_root)` and `find_ticket_file(repo_root, ulid)`.
4. **Create** `megaplan/tickets/core.py` — mode-aware dispatch:
   - `_get_store()` returns the configured store (mirror whatever `_core` already does for store detection — do not invent a new mechanism).
   - Each public op: always touches the file; if a store is configured, also calls the corresponding DB helper. On DB error in cloud mode: raise loudly (no `.pending/` queue this sprint).
   - `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, *, store=None, repo_root=None)`: file-update path always (walk `.megaplan/tickets/*.md`, flip frontmatter `status` to `addressed` for any file whose `epics:` list contains `epic_id` with `resolves_on_complete: true`); cloud path additionally calls `store.address_tickets_resolved_by_epic(epic_id)`. Idempotent: skip files whose status is already `addressed` or `dismissed`.
5. **Source derivation**: helper that reads `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env; if set, populates `source='agent'` + `filed_in_turn`; else `source='human'`.

### Step 6: CLI subcommand group (`megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Register** a new `ticket` subparser in `megaplan/cli.py` (mirror the `epic` block at ~line 1256) with verbs: `new`, `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`, `unlink`, `addressed`, `dismiss`, `reopen`. Add `--json` to read commands, `--tag/--status/--epic` filters to `list`, `--resolves` to `link`, `--note` to `addressed/dismiss`, `-b/--edit/-` to `new`.
2. **Create** `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py` with one handler per verb that calls into `megaplan.tickets` and renders output.
   - `ticket new`: writes only the ULID to stdout; everything else to stderr.
   - `ticket list`: local-only walks files in cwd; cloud mode queries DB (accepts `--codebase owner/name`).
3. **Wire** dispatch in the main `cli.py` command router.

### Step 7: Auto-address hook (`megaplan/store/db.py:960`, `megaplan/store/file.py:1054`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **In** `megaplan/store/db.py` `update_epic` — after the `status == "done"` branch sets `completed_at`, after the row is persisted, call `megaplan.tickets.address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, store=self)`. Wrap import locally to avoid circular imports.
2. **Mirror** the same call in `megaplan/store/file.py:1054`.
3. **Idempotency** comes from the helper itself; no extra guards needed here.

### Step 8: Planner discovery integration (`megaplan/handlers/plan.py` + planner prompt assembly)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Identify** the function that builds context for a new/refined epic (in `megaplan/handlers/plan.py` or `megaplan/prompts/`).
2. **Add** a section "Open tickets for this repo" listing the top N (e.g., 20) open tickets ranked by `tag overlap with epic goal/title desc, created_at desc`. Source: cloud mode → `store.list_tickets(codebase_id=…, status='open')`; local-only → `megaplan.tickets.list_open(repo_root)`.
3. **Mention** in the prompt that the planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true` (the actual linking still happens via `ticket link` after epic creation).

### Step 9: Resident/agent prompt (`megaplan/data/instructions.md` or `megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a short paragraph to the resident agent instructions: `megaplan ticket new "<title>" -b "<body>"` exists; use it to capture out-of-scope observations during epic work or to record user-requested bugs/notes. ULID is printed to stdout.

### Step 10: Tests (`tests/test_tickets.py`, extend `tests/test_cli_entry.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Local-only mode**: tmp git repo, no store configured. Cover `new` writes the file with correct frontmatter; `list` finds it; `link`/`unlink` updates frontmatter `epics:`; `addressed`/`reopen`/`dismiss` flip `status`; `address_resolved_by_epic` walks files and updates idempotently.
2. **Cloud-configured mode**: with an in-memory/sqlite-backed `DBStore` fixture (mirror existing `tests/conftest.py` patterns). Cover the same ops doing file + DB write-through; auto-register codebase on first call with `root_commit_sha`; `link` writes both file frontmatter and `ticket_epics` row.
3. **Auto-address**: epic transitions to `done` via `update_epic`; verify linked tickets flip to `addressed` in both file and DB; second call is a no-op.
4. **Codebase identity**: rename case (owner/name changes but `root_commit_sha` stable → same `codebase_id`); fresh-repo case (no commits → CLI errors cleanly).
5. **CLI happy paths**: `megaplan ticket new`, `list --json`, `link --resolves` round-trip via `subprocess` or the existing CLI test harness.

### Step 11: Validation
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the new test file first: `pytest tests/test_tickets.py -x`.
2. **Run** the broader suite focused on touched areas: `pytest tests/test_cli_entry.py tests/test_auto.py -x`.
3. **Run** the full suite: `pytest -x`.
4. **Smoke**: in a scratch git repo, `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID=t_test megaplan ticket new "test" -b "body" -t demo`; verify stdout is just the ULID, file exists with `source: agent`, `filed_in_turn: t_test`.

## Execution Order
1. Migration + schema models (Steps 2–3) — defines the data shape everything else builds on.
2. DB CRUD (Step 4) — add and unit-test in isolation.
3. `megaplan/tickets/` module (Step 5) — file path first, then mode dispatch.
4. CLI (Step 6) — exercises the module end-to-end.
5. Auto-address hook (Step 7).
6. Planner integration + agent prompt (Steps 8–9).
7. Tests + validation (Steps 10–11).

## Validation Order
1. Targeted ticket tests (Step 11.1).
2. CLI + epic-transition regression tests (Step 11.2).
3. Full suite (Step 11.3).
4. Manual smoke (Step 11.4).

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-plan-to-extract-the-20260419-1637"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-050",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "vendor-hermes-agent-into-the-20260422-0112"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-064",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: batch execute: recurring debt: the revised step 11 still has an implementation-ordering problem for high-drift blocking in `handle_execute_one_batch`. in the real code, `blocking_reasons` is converted to `blocked` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:594-601`, and only later does the final-batch block build and write `execution.json` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:611-621`. the plan says to compute final-batch drift after that aggregate write but also append the high-drift reason before `blocked = bool(blocking_reasons)` is evaluated, which is impossible without explicitly moving/restructuring the existing `blocked` decision and the `state[\"current_state\"] = state_executed` assignment.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint1-step-receipts"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-067",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new judge auto-selection rule against the actual profile format. profiles use agent specs parsed by `parse_agent_spec()`, such as `claude`, `codex`, and `hermes:moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`; the plan's ordered auto candidates mix agent names (`claude`, `codex`) with a bare model name (`gpt-5`) and only says to scan execute/plan assignments for appearances. without a precise normalization rule, auto-selection can miss a compared profile using `hermes:openai/gpt-5` and can also hand `run_judge` a bare `claude` or `codex` token that is an agent label in this repo, not the same kind of model identifier as `gpt-5`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint2-bakeoff"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-073",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: stop-signal persistence is internally inconsistent. step 5 adds `stop_signal` to `execution_doc.json` task updates, but only adds `stance` to `finalize.json` tasks; step 11 then says `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` reads latest `finalize.json` for `stance.stop_signal.requested`. in the current merge code, `_validate_and_merge_batch()` only copies fields listed in `merge_fields` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`), so a sibling `task_update.stop_signal` would not be present in `finalize.json` unless the plan explicitly adds it to the finalize schema and merge fields, or reads it from `execution.json`/`directors_notes.json` instead.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-074",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 11's pseudocode calls `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` from inside `_resolve_review_outcome()`, but the current `_resolve_review_outcome()` signature has no `plan_dir` parameter and both call sites pass only review counts, robustness, state, issues, and criteria (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:148-190`, `332-337`, `483-488`). the plan needs to specify adding `plan_dir` or pass precomputed stop-signal data into the resolver; otherwise the helper has no path to read finalize or directors-notes data.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-085",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the creative-light transition target is not technically executable as written. `workflow[state_gated]` has `transition(\"finalize\", state_finalized)` and `handle_finalize()` requires `state_gated`, but the proposed creative-light override is `state_planned: [transition(\"finalize\", state_gated)]`; no current handler applies that `next_state` before running `finalize`. `workflow_transition()` is only used for tiebreaker and revise/gate paths, while `_finish_step()` uses `workflow_next()` for display only, so this transition would produce an invalid next step from a planned state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname=\"hidden md:block\"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones.",

    },

      "id": "DEBT-012",

    },

      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-v2"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-047",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: issue hints: the plan now wires `mode=auto|chain|idle`, but the user-requested `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec` still have no deployment path into the container. reigh's readme uploads the idea file explicitly before running `megaplan init`, and `chain.sh` exits if `/workspace/chain.yaml` is missing, so a plan that only sets these paths in `cloud.yaml` without also staging the files still leaves the requested runner modes incomplete.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-071",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: creative workflow mapping: the brief says the prep phase chooses targeted provocations and that standard creative mode gets a full triad on prep, but the current workflow overrides standard robustness from initialized directly to plan in `megaplan/_core/workflow.py:78-82`. the plan adds creative prep prompt builders, yet it does not name a creative-specific workflow change to make standard `--mode creative` actually run prep.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

    {
      "id": "DEBT-072",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: stop-affordance: the brief's reason for the maker stop signal is to prevent additional passes from damaging the piece, but the plan says to halt further critique/revise cycles only when `stop_signal.requested=true` and the review verdict is approved. in the current review handler, an approved review already returns `state_done` with no next step in `_resolve_review_outcome()` (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:148-190`), so that condition is effectively a no-op and would not protect against a reviewer requesting rework despite the maker's stop defense.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the prior missing locations in `megaplan/workers.py` and `tests/test_workers.py` are now covered, but one supporting-infrastructure path is still absent from the steps: the repo-root generated schema copy under `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and the tests that read it directly. without either explicitly regenerating that file or changing those tests to materialize schemas in a temp root, the plan can still leave the runtime-schema copy out of sync with the raw registry.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-035",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan adds _set_active_step and _clear_active_step helpers to handlers.py. _run_worker (handlers.py:135) is modified to accept a `resolved` parameter. but _run_worker is also called directly by handle_critique's sequential fallback at line 801 and 803. these calls currently don't pass `resolved`. the plan's step 2.6 for handle_critique says to set active_step after resolution at line 794 \u2014 but the _run_worker calls at 801/803 would re-resolve the agent internally (since `resolved` isn't passed). this means the agent is resolved twice on the critique sequential path. the double-resolution is wasteful but correct (same result). however, to match the plan's goal of 'resolve_agent_mode() is called once per handler invocation', the resolved tuple should be passed to these _run_worker calls too.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-megaplan-s-planning-20260409-2103"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-048",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: supporting infrastructure is still missing for mode inputs. step 3 ports `chain.yaml.example` into package templates, but step 5's `materialize_deploy_dir()` layout does not include it, step 7 deploy does not upload it or `auto.idea_file`, and the docs/validation steps do not mention any artifact-sync path before boot. that leaves the runner depending on files that the deployment plan never places on the container volume.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-058",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the location coverage is still not literally complete in the plan's validation layer because step 3.4 and step 9.1 only search for `from run_agent import` and `from hermes_state import` statements. that leaves bare `import run_agent` or `import hermes_state` outside `megaplan/agent/` unguarded even though the current repo already uses that form in availability helpers, so the 'all locations by construction' claim remains overstated.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "vendor-hermes-agent-into-the-20260422-0112"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-069",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked the added judge tests in step 13.6. they cover omitted, auto, and explicit judge branches, but the described auto test says profiles whose execute/plan map covers `claude` and `codex` should select `codex` or a third-tier model; per the ordered list and exclusion rule, `codex` is already used and should not be a valid auto-selected judge in that scenario.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint2-bakeoff"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-077",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: generated/runtime schema infrastructure is a required location. `ensure_runtime_layout()` writes every schema from `schemas` to `.megaplan/schemas` (`megaplan/_core/io.py:306-312`), and worker invocations read those disk schemas via `schemas_root()` (`megaplan/workers.py:1289-1290`, `1395-1396`; `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:281-283`). the plan updates `schemas` but does not include a runtime-schema assertion for `execution_doc.json`/creative mode or any new sidecar schema, so schema additions may not be tested through the same path agents actually consume.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-078",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan says to keep `--mode joke` working as an alias while new plans set `mode='creative'` and `form='joke'`, but it does not include a migration/helper contract for existing state files where `mode='joke'` and no `form` exists outside prompt dispatch. current callers read `state['config'].get('mode')` directly across execute, review, workers, and cli status; without a shared `is_creative_mode()`/`creative_form_id()` helper, it is easy for back-compat to work in prompts but fail in execution or validation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-086",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the workflow location coverage is still incomplete for creative-light. the plan updates `workflow_next()` behavior and tests only `workflow_next()` assertions, but the runnable command path also depends on `handle_finalize()`'s `require_state(..., {state_gated})` check in `megaplan/handlers/finalize.py:211-213`. the plan should either include that handler/state transition change or adjust the workflow design so `finalize` is only surfaced while the state is actually `state_gated`.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i rechecked the caller paths after the revision. the raw-schema caller path through `validate_payload()` is now accounted for, but the runtime-schema caller path still is not: `strict_schema()` only reaches the on-disk runtime schemas through `ensure_runtime_layout()`, while current tests like `tests/test_schemas.py` and `tests/test_parallel_review.py` consume the already-written repo copy directly. the plan does not yet say how that caller path gets refreshed or asserted after the code change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-051",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the real callers for these paths are shell commands that expect actual files. reigh's readme uploads an idea file into `/workspace/megaplan-idea-2week.txt` before calling `megaplan init`, and `chain.sh` immediately checks `-f \"$spec\"` before running `megaplan chain --spec \"$spec\"`. the revised plan now constructs equivalent callers from `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec`, but still never adds the corresponding file-transfer or materialization step, so those callers would receive nonexistent arguments.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-065",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: batch execute callers still need a clearer identity contract. `handle_execute_one_batch()` can be invoked once per global batch in the same plan iteration, and the revised plan tells every invocation to use `phase=\"execute\"`, `iteration=state[\"iteration\"]`, and `attempt=1`. those actual caller arguments are not enough to distinguish batch receipts unless the plan changes the filename/attempt semantics or emits only one final aggregate receipt for `--batch` runs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint1-step-receipts"
      ]
    },

        "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: execute receipt identity: the plan introduces a new collision for multi-batch `--batch` runs. it says execute emits one receipt per handler invocation and `attempt` stays 1, while step 11 emits non-final and final batch receipts with the same `phase=\"execute\"` and `iteration=state[\"iteration\"]`; the plan-dir filename remains `step_receipt_execute_v<iteration>.json`. running batch 1 and then batch 2 in the same iteration would overwrite the plan-dir receipt and create multiple jsonl rows with the same `(plan_id, phase, iteration, attempt)`, contradicting the brief's one-receipt-per-identity contract.",

        Debt guidance:
        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.
        - When a concern is recurring debt that still needs to be flagged, prefix it with `Recurring debt:` after the subsystem tag, for example `Timeout recovery: Recurring debt: retry backoff remains brittle`.

Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

```
1	# Tickets
2	
3	A lightweight way to capture problems, bugs, or "we should look at this" notes against a repo, so they can later be folded into epics. Tickets are deliberately small: just enough structure to triage, link, and auto-close when the work that addresses them ships.
4	
5	## Why this is a new concept (not `feedback`)
6	
7	The existing `feedback` table holds guidance about *the tooling itself* — corrections to how the agent behaves, what to avoid, what worked. It is injected into prompts.
8	
9	Tickets are different: they describe *a problem in a codebase* that may become work. They are inert until folded into an epic. Keep the tables separate; no shared lifecycle.
10	
11	## Modes
12	
13	Megaplan runs in two modes; tickets must work in both.
14	
15	- **Local-only** — no cloud configured. The `.md` files on disk are the sole source of truth. No DB row, no codebase row. Identity is the repo's root commit SHA, computed on demand.
16	- **Cloud-configured** — DB store available. The `.md` files remain on disk and committed to the repo, and a mirrored row exists in `tickets` / `ticket_epics`. The DB is the system of record for live state; the file is the human-readable artifact.
17	
18	Mode detection follows the same rule megaplan core already uses (presence of a configured store). The CLI dispatches: if cloud is configured, write-through to DB and sync; otherwise file-only operations.
19	
20	Everything in the rest of this doc applies to both modes unless explicitly marked **(cloud-only)**.
21	
22	## Data model
23	
24	The tables below are **cloud-only**: they're created and used when a store is configured. In local-only mode, the equivalent state lives entirely in `.md` frontmatter (see [Local format](#local-format)).
25	
26	### New table: `tickets` (cloud-only)
27	
28	| column | type | notes |
29	|---|---|---|
30	| `id` | ULID | also the local filename stem |
31	| `codebase_id` | FK → `codebases.id` | required, primary scoping |
32	| `title` | text | required, ≤120 chars, one line |
33	| `body` | text | required, markdown, non-empty |
34	| `tags` | text[] | optional, freeform |
35	| `status` | enum | `open` \| `addressed` \| `dismissed`, default `open` |
36	| `source` | enum | `human` \| `agent` \| `post-mortem` |
37	| `filed_by_actor_id` | FK → actors | nullable |
38	| `filed_in_turn_id` | FK → `bot_turns` | nullable; populated when agent files mid-turn |
39	| `resolution_note` | text | filled on addressed/dismissed |
40	| `created_at`, `last_edited_at`, `addressed_at`, `dismissed_at` | timestamps | |
41	
42	### New table: `ticket_epics` (many-to-many, cloud-only)
43	
44	| column | type | notes |
45	|---|---|---|
46	| `ticket_id` | FK | |
47	| `epic_id` | FK | |
48	| `resolves_on_complete` | bool | **the key field** — does completing this epic close this ticket? |
49	| `linked_at`, `linked_by_actor_id`, `note` | | |
50	
51	Primary key `(ticket_id, epic_id)`. A ticket can be linked to N epics over its life; an epic can address N tickets.
52	
53	### Addition to `codebases` (cloud-only)
54	
55	Add `root_commit_sha` (text, indexed). Used as the durable repo identity — see [Codebase identity](#codebase-identity). Backfilled lazily for existing rows on first access. In local-only mode this column doesn't apply; the root SHA is computed directly from `git` when needed.
56	
57	That's the entire schema change. No new event/audit tables — provenance is covered by `filed_by_actor_id` / `filed_in_turn_id` and the local file's git history.
58	
59	## Local format
60	
61	One markdown file per ticket, **committed to the repo**:
62	
63	```
64	<repo>/.megaplan/tickets/{ulid}-{slug}.md
65	```
66	
67	The path establishes the repo association — no `codebase_id` in frontmatter.
68	
69	```markdown
70	---
71	id: 01HXY...
72	title: Execute step retries swallow stderr on timeout
73	tags: [execute, observability]
74	status: open
75	source: agent
76	filed_by: actor_01H...        # raw string; FK only resolved in cloud mode
77	filed_in_turn: turn_01H...    # raw string; FK only resolved in cloud mode
78	created_at: 2026-05-11T14:22:00Z
79	epics:
80	  - id: epic_01HZ...
81	    resolves_on_complete: true
82	---
83	
84	# Body
85	
86	Markdown prose describing the issue.
87	```
88	
89	Frontmatter is **complete in local-only mode** — it carries everything needed to reconstruct ticket state without a DB. In cloud mode, the same fields are mirrored to the `tickets` row; `filed_by` / `filed_in_turn` become real FKs (`filed_by_actor_id`, `filed_in_turn_id`). The `epics:` list mirrors the `ticket_epics` join in cloud mode and is the sole representation of links in local-only mode.
90	
91	## CLI
92	
93	One surface. The agent shells out to it; no separate tool API.
94	
95	```
96	megaplan ticket new "<title>" [-b "<body>" | --edit | -]  [-t tag1,tag2]
97	megaplan ticket list   [--tag X] [--status open] [--epic E]
98	megaplan ticket show   <id>
99	megaplan ticket edit   <id>                              # opens $EDITOR on the .md
100	megaplan ticket link   <ticket> <epic> [--resolves]
101	megaplan ticket unlink <ticket> <epic>
102	megaplan ticket addressed <id> [--note "..."]
103	megaplan ticket dismiss   <id> [--note "..."]
104	megaplan ticket reopen    <id>
105	```
106	
107	Conventions:
108	
109	- `--json` on any read command emits structured output.
110	- `ticket new` prints **only the ULID** to stdout on success (human chatter to stderr) so the agent can pipe/capture it trivially.
111	- `source` is auto-derived: if `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` is set in env, `source=agent` and `filed_by` / `filed_in_turn` are populated; otherwise `source=human`.
112	- All commands resolve the repo from cwd (see [Codebase identity](#codebase-identity)). In cloud mode this resolves to a `codebase_id` (auto-registering the row if needed). In local-only mode it's just the cwd repo; no registration step.
113	- `list` scope differs by mode: in local-only it lists files under `<cwd>/.megaplan/tickets/`. In cloud mode the same command can also accept `--codebase <owner>/<name>` to query across repos via the DB; without that flag it defaults to the current repo.
114	
115	## Lifecycle & auto-addressing
116	
117	1. **File** → `status=open`, written to `.md` and DB.
118	2. **Link to epic** → join row created, optionally `resolves_on_complete=true`.
119	3. **Epic completes** → for each ticket linked with `resolves_on_complete=true`, flip ticket to `addressed`, set `addressed_at`, fill `resolution_note` with `"Resolved by epic <id> on <date>: <epic title>"`. Idempotent — re-running on an already-addressed ticket is a no-op.
120	4. **Epic cancelled/dismissed** → ticket status is untouched. Link stays as history.
121	5. **Reopen** → `reopen` flips ticket back to `open`. The linking epic's join row has `resolves_on_complete` demoted to `false` and a note appended.
122	
123	Auto-addressing fires from the epic state-transition hook (same place that emits epic events). Add a single call after `state → done`: `address_tickets_resolved_by_epic(epic_id)`. The implementation has two paths, dispatched on mode:
124	
125	- **Cloud-configured** — updates `tickets` rows and the corresponding `.md` frontmatter in the working repo (if present).
126	- **Local-only** — walks `.megaplan/tickets/*.md`, finds files whose `epics:` list contains this epic with `resolves_on_complete: true`, and updates their frontmatter in place.
127	
128	Both paths are idempotent.
129	
130	## Discovery at planning time
131	
132	When a new epic is created or refined for a repo, surface the open tickets for that codebase (ranked by tag overlap with the epic goal + recency) into the planner prompt. The planner proposes which to link with `resolves_on_complete=true`. This is the natural "folding" moment — no separate ceremony.
133	
134	A standalone `megaplan ticket link` exists for ad-hoc linking outside that flow.
135	
136	## Codebase identity
137	
138	Durable key: the SHA of the repo's root commit (`git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`). Survives rename, transfer, fork, remote URL change.
139	
140	**Local-only mode**: compute the root SHA on demand. That's the identity. No row, no registration.
141	
142	**Cloud-configured mode**: stored as `codebases.root_commit_sha`. Resolution order on any ticket CLI invocation:
143	
144	1. Compute the root SHA from cwd.
145	2. Look up codebase by `root_commit_sha`. If found, use that `codebase_id`. If `(owner, name)` no longer matches the current remote, silently update those columns — the ID is preserved, all tickets keep working.
146	3. If not found, fall back to `(owner, name)` lookup (handles pre-existing rows without `root_commit_sha` — backfill it on hit).
147	4. If still not found, auto-register a new codebase row using current owner/name/remote and the root SHA, then proceed.
148	
149	Edge cases:
150	
151	- **No commits yet** → CLI refuses; tell the user to make their first commit.
152	- **Multiple root commits** (rare) → use the smallest SHA when sorted; document.
153	- **Submodule / subdir** → root SHA is the outer repo's. Tickets attach to the repo, not the subdir.
154	
155	## Sync model
156	
157	Mode-dependent:
158	
159	- **Local-only** — every operation writes the `.md` file. That's it. No queue, no sync, no DB.
160	- **Cloud-configured** — write-through with an offline fallback queue:
161	  - Every `ticket new/edit/link/...` writes the `.md` and pushes to DB in the same call.
162	  - If the DB write fails (offline, network error), the intent is stashed in `.megaplan/tickets/.pending/{ulid}.json`.
163	  - The next successful online command (or an explicit `megaplan ticket sync`) flushes the pending queue.
164	  - The `.md` files are the truth offline; the DB is the truth when reachable. Reconcile on flush by comparing `last_edited_at`.
165	
166	`megaplan ticket sync` is a no-op in local-only mode.
167	
168	## What this does NOT include (intentionally)
169	
170	- No severity field — tags cover prioritization for now.
171	- No assignment / owner — tickets are repo-scoped, not person-scoped.
172	- No comments / threads — edit the body or add a new ticket.
173	- No cross-repo tickets — one ticket, one codebase. Use a `cross-repo` tag if needed.
174	- No separate audit table — provenance is on the row + git history of the `.md`.
175	- No agent-facing tool API distinct from the CLI.
176	
177	If any of these become real pain points, revisit. Don't pre-build.
178	
179	## Integration touchpoints (what to change beyond the new tables)
180	
181	Follow the core-first principle: the local-file path lives in `_core` / `megaplan/tickets/`; DB persistence is layered on top and only kicks in when a store is configured.
182	
183	- New module `megaplan/tickets/` — the canonical local representation (read/write `.md` files, frontmatter parsing, link/address operations on files), plus mode-aware dispatch into the store. This module is what the CLI and the epic hook both call.
184	- `megaplan/store/db.py` **(cloud-only)**: add `tickets` and `ticket_epics` to the column allowlists; CRUD helpers (`create_ticket`, `list_tickets`, `link_ticket_to_epic`, `address_tickets_resolved_by_epic`, etc.); add `root_commit_sha` to `codebases` allowlist.
185	- Schema migration **(cloud-only)**: create the two tables; `ALTER TABLE codebases ADD COLUMN root_commit_sha text` with an index.
186	- `megaplan/cli.py`: register the `ticket` subcommand group. CLI handlers call into `megaplan/tickets/`, which internally decides whether to also touch the store.
187	- Epic state-transition hook (wherever an epic moves to `done`): call `megaplan.tickets.address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id)`. The function runs the file-update path in both modes, and the DB-update path additionally in cloud mode.
188	- Epic planner prompt assembly: when building context for a new/refined epic, include open tickets for the repo. In local-only mode this reads `.megaplan/tickets/*.md`; in cloud mode it queries the DB. Same shape of data, different source.
189	- Resident/agent prompt: a short paragraph telling the agent that `megaplan ticket new` exists and when to reach for it (out-of-scope observations during epic work, user-requested captures).
190	
191	## Open items
192	
193	- Exact ranking heuristic for "open tickets shown at planning time" — start with `tag overlap desc, created_at desc`, refine if it's noisy.
194	- Whether `ticket edit` should diff the new frontmatter against the DB and reject conflicting concurrent edits, or last-write-wins. Last-write-wins for now; add conflict detection only if it bites.
195
```

The real `update_epic` (db.py:584-614, file.py:729-744) performs a generic UPDATE with no status==done branch. Inserting the `address_resolved_by_epic` call at those line numbers as written would fire on every checklist-item completion, not epic completion, and would never fire on an actual epic transition to `done`. The plan picks `update_epic` as the single hook surface but never confirms that every code path that moves an epic to `done` goes through it.         Idea:
# Sprint brief — Tickets MVP

## Goal

Implement the tickets feature in megaplan per the design in `docs/tickets.md`. Tickets are short, repo-scoped notes on issues/problems, captured by humans or by agents on a human's prompt, that get folded into epics and auto-addressed when the resolving epic completes.

**The design doc `docs/tickets.md` is the source of truth for the data model, CLI surface, file format, modes, codebase identity, and integration touchpoints. Read it first. This brief only adds sprint scoping — what's in, what's out, what success looks like.**

## In scope for this sprint

Everything required to make tickets usable end-to-end in both **local-only mode** and **cloud-configured mode**:

1. **Schema migration (cloud-only)**: add `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables; add `root_commit_sha` column to `codebases` with an index. Additive only — no changes to existing tables beyond the new column.
2. **`megaplan/store/db.py`**: CRUD helpers for the new tables, column allowlist entries, codebase resolution that prefers `root_commit_sha`.
3. **New module `megaplan/tickets/`**: the canonical local representation. Frontmatter read/write for `.megaplan/tickets/{ulid}-{slug}.md` files. Mode-aware dispatch (cloud-configured → file + DB; local-only → file only). Codebase identity resolution from cwd via `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`.
4. **CLI `megaplan ticket …`** with all verbs in `docs/tickets.md` § CLI: `new`, `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`, `unlink`, `addressed`, `dismiss`, `reopen`. `ticket new` prints only the ULID to stdout on success; `--json` on read commands. `source` auto-derived from `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env.
5. **Auto-address hook**: in the epic state-transition path, after an epic moves to `done`, call `megaplan.tickets.address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id)`. Implementation handles both modes per the design doc. Idempotent.
6. **Planner discovery integration**: when assembling context for new/refined epics, include open tickets for the current repo, ranked by `tag overlap with epic goal desc, created_at desc`. The planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`. Same data shape in both modes (DB query in cloud, file scan in local-only).
7. **Resident/agent prompt**: a short paragraph teaching the agent that `megaplan ticket new` exists and when to use it (out-of-scope observations during epic work, user-requested captures).
8. **Tests** covering both modes for: file ↔ DB round-trip, link/unlink, auto-address idempotence, codebase identity resolution (rename case, fresh-repo case), CLI happy paths.

## Out of scope (defer)

- **Offline queue / `.pending/` directory / `megaplan ticket sync`**. Write-through only for now; if the DB write fails in cloud mode, the CLI errors loudly and the user retries. The design doc describes the offline queue — explicitly **do not build it** this sprint. Build the minimal write-through path.
- Conflict detection on concurrent edits — last-write-wins is fine.
- Severity, assignment, comments, cross-repo tickets — explicitly excluded per the design doc.
- Anything described in the doc's **Open items** section.

## Success criteria

- `megaplan ticket new "title" -b "body"` works in a local-only repo and writes the `.md` file.
- The same command in a cloud-configured repo writes the `.md` file **and** inserts a `tickets` row, auto-registering the codebase if needed (with `root_commit_sha` populated).
- `megaplan ticket link <ticket> <epic> --resolves` updates both the file frontmatter and the DB join row.
- Marking an epic `done` flips the linked ticket's status to `addressed` and writes a `resolution_note` referencing the epic — in both modes.
- New epic creation surfaces open tickets for the repo in the planner's context, ranked.
- Tests pass for both modes.

## Notes for the planner

- Follow the **core-first principle**: `megaplan/tickets/` owns the capability; the DB layer is purely additive and only kicks in when a store is configured. Mode detection follows whatever mechanism megaplan core already uses (presence of a configured store) — do not invent a new one.
- The `tickets` concept is **distinct from `feedback`**. Do not reuse the `feedback` table; do not conflate their lifecycles.
- Vendor flags / FK conventions / migration patterns should match how existing tables are handled in `megaplan/store/db.py`. Read existing CRUD helpers before designing new ones.
- The auto-address hook integration point is the one place where you must touch existing epic state-transition code. Find the existing hook surface (where epic events are emitted on state change) and add the call there — don't invent a parallel transition path.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Tickets MVP

## Overview
Add a repo-scoped tickets feature per `docs/tickets.md`. Build a new `megaplan/tickets/` module that owns the canonical local representation, wire mode-aware dispatch into the DB store, register a `megaplan ticket ...` CLI subcommand group, and inject one auto-address call into the place where epic state actually transitions to `done`. DB changes are additive: new `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables plus a `root_commit_sha` column on `codebases`. No `.pending/` queue this sprint.

Verified repo facts (corrections from prior plan):
- `update_epic` (`megaplan/store/db.py:584-614`, `megaplan/store/file.py:729-744`) is a generic UPDATE on arbitrary `**changes` and has no `state == 'done'` branch today. The prior `:960`/`:1054` lines were inside `update_checklist_item`.
- The Epic state column is `state` (not `status`).
- Epic state transitions are recorded via `store.record_epic_event` (`db.py:2295`, called from `megaplan/editorial/_events.py:63` and `megaplan/editorial/gating.py:213`).
- `create_codebase`/`upsert_codebase` INSERTs (`db.py:1797-1811`, `db.py:1830+`) hard-enumerate columns; allowlist alone will not persist `root_commit_sha`.
- No `cloud_configured` predicate exists in `_core` today. Plan defines `megaplan.tickets.is_cloud_store(store) := isinstance(store, DBStore)` as the canonical signal.
- `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` is not exported by the agent runtime today; the resident loop must export it for `source='agent'` to function.

## Phase 1: Schema + Types

### Step 1: Audit (`megaplan/store/db.py`, `megaplan/store/file.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/plan.py`, `megaplan/editorial/_events.py`, `megaplan/editorial/gating.py`, `megaplan/resident/runtime.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Read codebase CRUD (`db.py:1779-1955`) and allowlists (`db.py:237-238`).
2. Map every code path that drives an epic to `state='done'`: `update_epic`, `record_epic_event`, editorial gating call sites, and any wrappers in `megaplan/store/multi.py`/`megaplan/store/compat.py`.
3. Locate the resident subprocess-spawn site in `megaplan/resident/runtime.py:167` for env wiring.
4. Skim `cli.py:1256` (`epic` subparser) for the established pattern.
5. Locate the planner context-assembly site in `megaplan/handlers/plan.py`.

### Step 2: Schema migration (`supabase/migrations/2026MMDDHHMM_tickets.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Additive migration: ALTER `codebases` ADD COLUMN `root_commit_sha text` + index; CREATE TABLE `tickets` and CREATE TABLE `ticket_epics` per the design doc.
2. FK column types are explicitly `text` for actors/turns/epics references; `codebases.id` remains `uuid`.

### Step 3: Schema dataclasses (`megaplan/schemas/models.py`, `megaplan/schemas/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Add `Ticket` and `TicketEpicLink` models; export.
2. Add `root_commit_sha: str | None = None` to `Codebase`.

## Phase 2: DB Store

### Step 4: DB store CRUD (`megaplan/store/db.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `_IDEMPOTENT_MUTATORS` (`db.py:89`) and exports (`db.py:114`) with the new helper names.
2. Extend column allowlists (`db.py:237-238`): add `root_commit_sha` to `codebases`; add `tickets` and `ticket_epics` entries.
3. **Extend `create_codebase` and `upsert_codebase` INSERT SQL** (`db.py:1797-1811`, `db.py:1830+`) to include `root_commit_sha` in column list, VALUES, and parameter array. Add `root_commit_sha: str | None = None` to both signatures. For `upsert_codebase`, also add to the `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE SET` clause.
4. Implement: `create_ticket`, `load_ticket`, `list_tickets`, `update_ticket` (no revision; LWW), `link_ticket_to_epic` (INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE), `unlink_ticket_from_epic`, `list_ticket_epic_links`, and `address_tickets_resolved_by_epic(epic_id) -> list[str]` (single SQL UPDATE filtered on `resolves_on_complete=true AND status='open'`, RETURNING ids; idempotent).
5. `resolve_codebase_by_root_sha(*, root_sha, owner, name, repo_url, default_branch) -> str`: prefer `root_commit_sha`; fall back to `(owner,name)` and backfill; else create with `root_commit_sha` populated.

## Phase 3: Tickets Module

### Step 5: New module `megaplan/tickets/`
**Scope:** Medium
1. `megaplan/tickets/__init__.py` exports public API plus `is_cloud_store`.
2. `megaplan/tickets/identity.py`: `repo_root_sha` via `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD` (clean error on no-commits; sort-and-pick on multiple roots); `repo_owner_name`; `repo_default_branch`.
3. `megaplan/tickets/files.py`: tickets dir, frontmatter read/write via `pyyaml`, slugify, iterators.
4. `megaplan/tickets/core.py`: **`is_cloud_store(store) -> bool := isinstance(store, DBStore)`** — canonical predicate. `_resolve_store()` reuses megaplan's existing factory. Each public op writes the file first; if cloud, also calls the DB helper. DB error in cloud mode raises loudly. `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, *, store=None, repo_root=None)`: `store` is the **resolved tickets/cloud store**, not the epic-event caller's `self`. File path always; cloud path additional.
5. `_derive_source()` reads `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env (set -> agent + filed_in_turn; unset -> human). `MEGAPLAN_ACTOR_ID` populates filed_by_actor_id when present.
6. Stdin body: `new(...)` accepts `body='-'` to read `sys.stdin.read()`; rejects empty.

## Phase 4: CLI + Hooks

### Step 6: CLI subcommand group (`megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Register `ticket` subparser (mirror `epic` block at `cli.py:1256`): `new <title> [-b BODY | --edit | -] [-t TAGS]`, `list [filters] [--json]`, `show`, `edit`, `link [--resolves]`, `unlink`, `addressed`, `dismiss`, `reopen`. Body source is a mutually exclusive required group; `-` reads stdin via `sys.stdin.read()` and rejects empty.
2. `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py` — one handler per verb. `ticket new` writes only ULID + newline to stdout; chatter to stderr.
3. Wire dispatch in `cli.py` router.

### Step 7: Auto-address hook (`megaplan/store/db.py`, `megaplan/store/file.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **In `update_epic`** (`db.py:584`, `file.py:729`), after the row is persisted, gate on `changes.get('state') == 'done'`. When true, call `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, store=_resolve_store(), repo_root=os.getcwd())`. The store passed is the resolved tickets/cloud store, not `self`.
2. **In `record_epic_event`** (`db.py:2295`), add the same gated call when the recorded event is a transition to `state='done'` (check `event_type` and `post_state.get('state')`).
3. Audit `megaplan/store/multi.py` and `megaplan/store/compat.py` wrappers; hook there or normalize the key if they reshape `changes['state']`.
4. Idempotency comes from the helper itself.

### Step 8: Planner discovery integration (`megaplan/handlers/plan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Identify the new/refined-epic context-assembly function.
2. Add an "Open tickets for this repo" section (top 20) ranked **strictly per the brief**: `tag overlap with epic GOAL desc, created_at desc` — overlap source is the epic goal text only, not title. Cloud -> `store.list_tickets(codebase_id, status='open')`; local-only -> `megaplan.tickets.list_open(repo_root)`.
3. Prompt mentions the planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`; the actual linking happens via the explicit CLI.

### Step 9: Resident/agent wiring + prompt (`megaplan/resident/runtime.py`, `megaplan/data/instructions.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Export `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID=<turn.id>` (and `MEGAPLAN_ACTOR_ID` when available) in subprocess env at `megaplan/resident/runtime.py:167`.
2. Add a short paragraph to `megaplan/data/instructions.md`: when to use `megaplan ticket new`; ULID is printed to stdout.

## Phase 5: Tests + Validation

### Step 10: Tests (`tests/test_tickets.py`, `tests/test_tickets_cli.py`, extend `tests/test_cli_entry.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Local-only mode: `new`, `new -b -` (stdin), `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`/`unlink`, status transitions, `address_resolved_by_epic` walks files idempotently.
2. Cloud-configured mode (DBStore fixture): same ops do file + DB write-through; `link --resolves` writes both.
3. Codebase auto-registration: first `ticket new` in a fresh repo creates a `codebases` row with `root_commit_sha` populated **NON-NULL**.
4. Auto-address hook: drive an epic to `state='done'` via `update_epic` AND via the editorial-gating path that calls `record_epic_event`; verify both file and DB flip; second transition is a no-op. A non-`done` `update_epic` call does NOT trigger the hook.
5. Identity: rename case (same root SHA -> same codebase_id); fresh-repo case (no commits -> CLI errors cleanly).
6. CLI happy paths via subprocess: `ticket new` stdout is exactly the ULID + newline; `list --json` valid JSON; `link --resolves` round-trip.

### Step 11: Validation
**Scope:** Small
1. `pytest tests/test_tickets.py tests/test_tickets_cli.py -x`.
2. `pytest tests/test_cli_entry.py tests/test_auto.py -x`.
3. `pytest -x`.
4. Smoke: `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID=t_test megaplan ticket new "test" -b "body" -t demo` in a scratch repo; verify stdout is just the ULID; file has `source: agent`, `filed_in_turn: t_test`.

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 (migration + models).
2. Phase 2 (DB CRUD + extended `create_codebase`/`upsert_codebase` INSERTs).
3. Phase 3 (`megaplan/tickets/` with `is_cloud_store`).
4. Phase 4: CLI -> hooks in both `update_epic` and `record_epic_event` -> planner + agent env.
5. Phase 5: tests + validation.

## Validation Order
1. Targeted ticket tests.
2. CLI + epic-transition regression.
3. Full suite.
4. Manual smoke.

      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-029",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-v2"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-032",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-046",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-plan-to-extract-the-20260419-1637"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-050",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "vendor-hermes-agent-into-the-20260422-0112"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-064",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: batch execute: recurring debt: the revised step 11 still has an implementation-ordering problem for high-drift blocking in `handle_execute_one_batch`. in the real code, `blocking_reasons` is converted to `blocked` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:594-601`, and only later does the final-batch block build and write `execution.json` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:611-621`. the plan says to compute final-batch drift after that aggregate write but also append the high-drift reason before `blocked = bool(blocking_reasons)` is evaluated, which is impossible without explicitly moving/restructuring the existing `blocked` decision and the `state[\"current_state\"] = state_executed` assignment.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint1-step-receipts"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-067",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new judge auto-selection rule against the actual profile format. profiles use agent specs parsed by `parse_agent_spec()`, such as `claude`, `codex`, and `hermes:moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`; the plan's ordered auto candidates mix agent names (`claude`, `codex`) with a bare model name (`gpt-5`) and only says to scan execute/plan assignments for appearances. without a precise normalization rule, auto-selection can miss a compared profile using `hermes:openai/gpt-5` and can also hand `run_judge` a bare `claude` or `codex` token that is an agent label in this repo, not the same kind of model identifier as `gpt-5`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint2-bakeoff"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-073",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: stop-signal persistence is internally inconsistent. step 5 adds `stop_signal` to `execution_doc.json` task updates, but only adds `stance` to `finalize.json` tasks; step 11 then says `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` reads latest `finalize.json` for `stance.stop_signal.requested`. in the current merge code, `_validate_and_merge_batch()` only copies fields listed in `merge_fields` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`), so a sibling `task_update.stop_signal` would not be present in `finalize.json` unless the plan explicitly adds it to the finalize schema and merge fields, or reads it from `execution.json`/`directors_notes.json` instead.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-074",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 11's pseudocode calls `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` from inside `_resolve_review_outcome()`, but the current `_resolve_review_outcome()` signature has no `plan_dir` parameter and both call sites pass only review counts, robustness, state, issues, and criteria (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:148-190`, `332-337`, `483-488`). the plan needs to specify adding `plan_dir` or pass precomputed stop-signal data into the resolver; otherwise the helper has no path to read finalize or directors-notes data.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-085",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the creative-light transition target is not technically executable as written. `workflow[state_gated]` has `transition(\"finalize\", state_finalized)` and `handle_finalize()` requires `state_gated`, but the proposed creative-light override is `state_planned: [transition(\"finalize\", state_gated)]`; no current handler applies that `next_state` before running `finalize`. `workflow_transition()` is only used for tiebreaker and revise/gate paths, while `_finish_step()` uses `workflow_next()` for display only, so this transition would produce an invalid next step from a planned state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname=\"hidden md:block\"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-012",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still contradicts itself on mobile behavior. assumption #3 says the browse-mode mobile score toggle will be preserved, but step 4.9 removes the entire mobile sticky nav that contains that toggle in `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:314-330`. the implementer cannot satisfy both instructions as written.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-028",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked step 7 against the brief's explicit prompt-builder requirement. the revision fixes the old context leak by introducing a brand-new `heavy_criteria_review_prompt`, but the brief said `_review_prompt()` should stay as-is and still run as the `criteria_verdict` check in heavy mode; v3 solves the problem by replacing that heavy-mode criteria path instead of reusing the baseline review prompt, which is still a material divergence from the requested design.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-v2"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-047",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: issue hints: the plan now wires `mode=auto|chain|idle`, but the user-requested `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec` still have no deployment path into the container. reigh's readme uploads the idea file explicitly before running `megaplan init`, and `chain.sh` exits if `/workspace/chain.yaml` is missing, so a plan that only sets these paths in `cloud.yaml` without also staging the files still leaves the requested runner modes incomplete.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-071",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: creative workflow mapping: the brief says the prep phase chooses targeted provocations and that standard creative mode gets a full triad on prep, but the current workflow overrides standard robustness from initialized directly to plan in `megaplan/_core/workflow.py:78-82`. the plan adds creative prep prompt builders, yet it does not name a creative-specific workflow change to make standard `--mode creative` actually run prep.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
